# Phase 4c/4d report: current-scenario re-runs, dwell sweep, second-review checks A-D

> Historical record: the `main.tex` line numbers in this report refer to an earlier paper version, not the current `paper/main.tex`.

Generated by `scripts/make_phase4c_report.py` at `bce29ea`, compared with `paper/numbers.tex` at `dcd2050` (Phase 4b).  Do not edit by hand.

## 0. Scope

- Geometric 2 deg screen (`ThetaColo*`, `ThetaWorstPeakPct`): now read from the as-deployed Gen2 E5 run (8 non-overlapping days) instead of E1 (Gen1 Walker).
- E7 (analytic model) and E8 (its diagnosis) on the FCC 22-91 Gen2 Walker shells, compared with the as-filed Gen2 E4 run.
- E9 (OneWeb 55 deg mask diagnosis) on the 2026-10-20 snapshot, Gen2 Starlink, 8 days 24 h apart.
- E5 Gen2 re-run with the dwell-time sweep added (N = 20/30/40 s for event-avoiding; N = 10..60 s for rate-maximizing).
- Fig. 2: no handoff, event-avoiding and rate-maximizing curves; right panel from -0.05.  The Fig. 2 caption is the only text change in `paper/main.tex`.

## 2. Dwell-time sweep (Gen2 as deployed, 8 non-overlapping days)

OneWeb victim, realistic variant, 0.45 m; worst qualified station per day, mean +/- 95% t-interval over days; 'over' = station-days above the limit.  Verdict: 'exceeds' / 'within' only when the whole interval is above / below the limit.  N = 10 s equals the simulation step.

## 6. B: handoff interruption cost (post-processing)

Run `results/e14_interruption_cost_20261021T204021Z_seed0/` (`scripts/interruption_cost.py`) on `results/e5_as_deployed_20261021T174655Z_seed0/`; no re-simulation.

### 6.1 Exact definition

For station g, day d (one row of `raw_handoff.parquet`), T_d = 86 400 s, step dt = 10 s, served(t) = the victim has a satellite above its mask:

- Policy switches: S_p(g,d) = #{t : s_p(t) != s_p(t - dt), both steps served} / (T_d / 3600) [1/h], where s_p(t) is the satellite the policy uses (`switches_all_per_hour`; `ngsi.dtt_screen.count_switches`).  A gap (an unserved step) between two satellites is not counted as a switch.
- Baseline switches: S_0(g,d), the same count for s_0(t) = the highest-elevation satellite (the unconstrained interference-free baseline that defines R_0), `base_switches_per_hour`.  It is the same baseline for every policy, including the dwell-constrained ones.
- Extra switches: E(g,d) = max(0, S_p - S_0).  This is a **count difference**, not a match of switch instants: a policy switch at a time step where the baseline also switches is not removed individually; only the excess of the totals is charged.
- Interrupted fraction of time: f(g,d; tau) = E(g,d) * tau / 3600.
- Availability change: dU_tau(g,d) = dU(g,d) + f(g,d; tau), with dU = #{t : served, C/N_0(t) >= gamma_0 and gamma_p(t) < gamma_0} / n (gamma_0 = 0 dB; C/N_0 is the baseline satellite's clear-sky C/N, gamma_p the policy's SINR; n the number of steps), the simulated `unavail_incr_div_frac`.  **Overlap is not removed**: an interruption is added even if it falls on a step that is already unavailable (gamma < gamma_0), baseline-unavailable (C/N_0 < 0 dB) or unserved, so dU_tau is an upper bound on the added unavailability for the stated tau.
- Throughput degradation: D_tau(g,d) = 1 - (1 - D(g,d)) (1 - f(g,d; tau)), i.e. R_tau = R_p (1 - f) with R_p the policy's **time-averaged** rate over the day (including its own outages), not the instantaneous rate at the switch instant; D = 1 - R_p / R_0 is the simulated `degradation_div` against the same baseline R_0.
- Order: tau is applied to every station-day first; then, per day, the worst qualified station (baseline availability >= 99%) is taken, and the reported value is the mean +/- 95% t-interval of those daily worsts over the days, as for every other 25.261(d) number.  'Over' counts station-days above the limit.
- Largest tau meeting a limit: bisection on [0, 60] s for the condition mean + CI < limit (both D_tau and dU_tau increase with tau).
